The user created a free zone on `dynv6.com`, of the `dns.army` kind, and steered through it: `STEERING TEST` became an attributed note within one poll interval, a second edit thirty seconds later became a second note, and a third arrived as a 370-character value with its blank lines and multiple character-strings intact, carrying three instructions that this entry and the prune below obey. `dns.army` is itself NXDOMAIN at its apex while `steering.vlht-mrsv.dns.army` resolves, because each hostname is its own delegated zone with `SOA ns1.dynv6.net`, so a dead apex does not mean a dead channel and `REFERENCE.md` now says how to check a candidate name before waiting on it.
- Running it live found the defect that mattered most. Forty queries of one name returned the current value 22 times and the previous value 18 times, six minutes after the edit and well past a 60s TTL, because the resolver serves more than one cache and they disagree. The poller read each flip as a change and delivered twelve duplicate notes before the real one arrived. Values are now deduplicated by digest, the digests are written to the log and recovered on restart, and a value already delivered is never delivered again, which also means re-setting an old note does nothing and one character has to change to send it again. Verified against the live oscillation: 25 polls delivered one note, and a warm restart that recovered the digest from the log delivered nothing.
- Two more defects came from the same live run. A transient NXDOMAIN on a name that had just delivered three notes made the startup banner announce that no note could ever arrive, so `probe_startup` now retries NXDOMAIN up to three times before believing it and says so when the resolver changed its mind, which four checks pin: a lie retried into the truth, a genuinely dead name still warning, a healthy name costing one query and no sleep, and the live name answering on the first attempt. And `query_txt` had returned an empty string both for a name with no TXT record and for a resolver that never answered, so one transient failure moved the baseline and the next answer replayed the whole record as a note; `None` now means no answer, the baseline survives it, and a baseline can be established late when the startup query itself failed.
- The note path was consolidated as the transports multiplied, and it is what survived them. `digest`, `added_lines`, `deliver`, and `DIRECTIVES` live in `scripts/steering_notes.py` with the header, the append, the tail cap, and the log write, so no poller imports another and the note format cannot drift. Running it found a defect reading did not: `NOTES_HEADER` ends in a newline that `split("## Current Notes:")[1]` returns, so every append re-added it and blank lines accumulated above the first note, nine after seven comments, eating the tail budget. The remainder is stripped on append and the assert demo pins the head gap at one. The log stamp now carries one timestamp instead of two and puts the body on its own line.
- A GitHub transport was built first, verified live, and then removed on instruction. It watched one issue or pull request through `api.github.com`, by default its description, so a steer was an edit that left no comment, commit, or notification, with comment mode opt-in and edit-aware, `If-None-Match` polling that made an unchanged poll free against the 5,400-request hourly limit, and a prefix diff that body mode and the DNS poller now share. It ran live against pull request 13, where a description edit became an attributed note within 12s and restoring it produced none, and against 148 real comments on `octocat/Hello-World#1`. The permissions it was built around are recorded because they outlive it: an Arena installation token can read issues but not create them, `POST /repos/{owner}/{name}/issues` answering 403 `Resource not accessible by integration`, can read and write pull request descriptions, and can write contents. Gists were checked twice over and are impossible here rather than merely declined: both gist hosts are TLS-closed, and `api.github.com/gists` answers 403 for GET, POST, and PATCH alike, since installation tokens have no gists access at all.
- The dropped skill's own ntfy channel left with it, on the instruction to omit the transports that failed or were declined. `scripts/external_steering.py` cannot run where it was written for, since `ntfy.sh` is TLS-closed here, though it works in the regular workspace it came from and nothing about it was defective; `scripts/generate_topic.py` and `scripts/install.sh` existed only to mint and advertise a topic, so they went too, and with them the two findings they carried, the installer's QR step failing silently into a missing `assets/` directory and the poller overwriting `STEERING.md` instead of appending. `REFERENCE.md` keeps a table of every channel measured and not shipped, with the evidence, so the next session does not re-try any of them, and it records that `pypi.org` and `registry.npmjs.org` would both work as note carriers and were rejected as one publish per steer.
- Root `AGENTS.md` gained verification item 4, Arena-scoped on request, and it earned its place inside the session: the sandbox was re-cloned mid-task, which dropped the local commits, the venv, and the seeded `tiktoken` cache, and the note rebuilt the last two verbatim on the first try while `git fetch` plus `git reset --mixed` against the pushed branch head restored the first. Install the two dependencies into a venv, because system `pip` refuses under PEP 668, and seed `TIKTOKEN_CACHE_DIR` from `niieani/gpt-tokenizer` `data/cl100k_base.tiktoken`, fetched by `gh api -H "Accept: application/vnd.github.raw"` against that blob and saved under the first 40 hex of the `sha1` of the `cl100k_base` blob URL, because `openaipublic.blob.core.windows.net` and `raw.githubusercontent.com` are unreachable while `api.github.com` answers. The item now carries the egress map, the DNS finding, and the one-way limit, so a future session picks a channel from measurement instead of rediscovering any of it.
- The user's side of the channel now has a page rather than a provider's form. `assets/steer.html` is one file with no dependencies: a textarea that autosaves 1.2s after typing stops, and a sync light driven by DNS-over-HTTPS instead of by the provider's API, so it reports what a poller can actually resolve rather than what the provider claims to have stored. It samples Google's resolver three times, the same network the sandbox's 8.8.8.8 sits on and so the best predictor of what the poller sees, and Cloudflare's three times as an independent check; goes amber and says so when the samples disagree, which is the two-cache condition measured below; tells NXDOMAIN apart from a name that resolves with no TXT record, since the fixes differ; and falls back to a copyable `curl` when the browser is not permitted to call the API, with the light still working because verification never needed it. The token stays in that browser's localStorage and goes only to the provider. Its TXT parsing is unit-tested against eleven payload shapes, including a value split at 255 octets and the `\DDD` presentation escapes a first draft got wrong by assuming JSON decoding had already handled them, and the embedded script passes `node --check`. Not exercised in a browser, since this sandbox can reach neither DoH endpoint nor the provider.
- The channel then took itself down, which is the finding that moved a default. After roughly 500 queries of one name in 25 minutes, at 8-10s polling plus four ad-hoc test loops, every name under that zone began answering NOERROR with no records of any type, including names that had never been queried and the zone's own SOA, while random names under three other zones still returned NXDOMAIN correctly and every control name still resolved. The zone stayed delegated and went empty, and the user had deleted nothing. Whether that is provider-side throttling or a policy in the sandbox's resolver path cannot be told from inside, so it is recorded as an open hazard rather than a solved one: `POLL_INTERVAL` now defaults to 30s instead of 10s, and `REFERENCE.md` carries the measurement with the rule to check the provider's panel or DoH from a browser before blaming the poller, and to treat a suddenly empty zone as a rate limit to wait out.
- A finding this changelog published two entries ago is retracted here, because a second measurement contradicted it. The claim was that a free provider's zone empties under query volume, based on a sweep where every name under the zone answered NOERROR with no records while other zones still returned NXDOMAIN and every control resolved. Ten queries of the same record later returned its value five times and no-data five times, each answer arriving inside two milliseconds, with `_dmarc.google.com`, the zone apex, and `example.com` correct on every attempt. The zone was never empty and the user deleted nothing; the sandbox's resolver path blinks on roughly half of all queries, and the earlier sweep happened to sample a run of blinks. The poller now queries a control name before saying anything about the record and reports which failure it is looking at, so an observer's blink can no longer be published as a fact about somebody's zone.
- Two channels previously written off are readable, and the distinction that hid them is worth stating exactly: the egress allowlist binds processes in the sandbox, not the agent's page-fetch path. Through that path a public gist's raw content, a DNS-over-HTTPS answer from `dns.google`, and an `ntfy.sh` topic page all loaded, while the sandbox's own sockets are closed to all three. The reliable read of the steering record is now `https://dns.google/resolve?name=<record>&type=TXT` at a turn boundary, and `REFERENCE.md` carries the procedure plus the caveat that no background poller can use a path only the agent can take. ntfy's JSON form returned HTTP 500 on an empty topic and has not been retried against a real message, so it is recorded as reachable rather than as working.
- The distribution workflow now carries the skill and not only the rules, which closes a gap that would have shipped silently: `distribute-arena.yml` pushed `rules/ARENA.md` to four repositories, and those rules now say to always activate `skills/arena-live-steering`, so a session in any other target would have been told to use a skill that was never delivered there. The workflow mirrors the tracked files under `skills/arena-live-steering` into each target's `.agents/skills/arena-live-steering`, clearing the destination first so a file deleted upstream cannot survive, taking the list from `git ls-files` rather than from disk so a local build artifact cannot ride along into four repositories, and verifying the committed file list and every file byte for byte, which is the check the `ARENA.md` half already had. Tested by extracting the workflow's own script from the YAML and running it against a seeded local target: the stale file was deleted, all seven files verified identical, the delivered `ARENA.md` matched its source, and a second run committed nothing.
- A rule amendment was only half applied, and the gate that should have caught it cannot. The house procedure in `rules/README.md` is to amend `rules/refs/` first and then mirror the amendment into the live file in compressed form, and the first pass did only the first half, so the turn-ending steering check existed in the uncompressed original and not in the `rules/ARENA.md` that actually gets uploaded. `check.py` passed throughout, because its refs/live parity rule checks only that a live section's rule count stays at or below its refs counterpart, which a refs-only addition satisfies by construction. Mirrored now, in both places the live file stated the open prompt it replaces, and the root byte-identical copy followed.
- The ntfy route gained continuous capture without the sandbox ever contacting ntfy, which is the property the DNS fallback was being kept for. `scripts/ntfy_relay.py` serves a page through the Arena preview proxy; the browser that opens it can reach `ntfy.sh` even though no process here can, so the browser subscribes to the topic's stream and forwards each message to the server, which writes it to the notes file with the same id deduplication as the manual path. If the stream is refused, most likely by CORS, the page polls the same topic every 10s instead, which needs no permission the stream did not. Closing the tab costs nothing: ntfy holds 12 hours of messages and `since=all` returns them, so the agent's own fetch at a turn boundary stays a complete read. Tested against a live server rather than read: a POST delivered, the same id posted again was skipped, and an NDJSON body carrying a `keepalive` event and a titled message delivered one note with its title intact and its directive echoed.
- `skills/arena-live-steering/SKILL.md` is measured in bytes rather than `cl100k_base` tokens, on the user's instruction, and the trade is worth stating: a byte count needs no tokenizer, so it survives a sandbox where the `tiktoken` cache cannot be seeded, which is the failure that made this repository's AGENTS.md carry a seeding procedure at all. What is given up is that tokens measure what a model pays to activate a skill and bytes only approximate it, at roughly four to one for English prose, and the other rows still need the tokenizer, so the dependency stays.
- The dailies automation no longer tracks LiteLLM, which its own section 4 said to remove once reported. Out go the tracked-PR section, the row in section 1, the report's `Watched` output that existed only for it with its two column definitions and its example row, and the item count in the prose follows the table down to ten, since a count that disagrees with its own table is how a monitoring list rots. What is left is consistent rather than merely shorter: section 5 becomes section 4, the four cross-references that named it follow, and the work order drops from `1, 4, 2, 3` to `1, 2, 3`, which still puts the expensive repository audit last.
- The check cadence gained a closing rule on the user's instruction: read the topic before the turn ends as well as at its start. The reason is not symmetry but the acknowledgment clause, which cannot fire on a note nobody has read, so without a closing check a note sent while the agent was working is delivered a turn late and acknowledged later still, which from the sending side is indistinguishable from a dropped message.
- Ntfy became the default route and DNS the fallback, on the user's measurement rather than the agent's: they published to a topic and edited a record in the same minute, and the ntfy note was instant while the record waited on a 60-second TTL and a resolver path that serves no data on half of all queries. Seven messages read in one poll, ingested, with a second ingest delivering nothing, settled the read path, and the payload's own `expires` field put retention at 12.0 hours instead of leaving it guessed. The check cadence follows from what a read costs: a network round trip inside the turn, so at every turn boundary and before anything hard to undo, rather than the every-tool-calls habit a local file makes cheap.
- `assets/steer.html` is deleted: 597 lines whose sending half could never work in a browser, since the provider answers a CORS preflight with an empty `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` and its GET update endpoint carries only `ipv4` and `ipv6`, and whose verifying half went redundant the moment ntfy's own UI confirmed a post instantly. The DNS route keeps the provider's panel and the one-line API call, which `REFERENCE.md` retains with the quoting test that proved it byte-exact.
- Session reports reached the remote and were rewritten out of it. Five `docs(reports)` commits went up as ancestors of later pushes, which is exactly what "commit locally, never push" does once any descendant is pushed, so the pattern was broken by construction rather than by a slip. `git filter-branch` stripped `reports/` from every branch commit, `--prune-empty` dropped the five, and the force-push left a tree verified byte-identical apart from those files, with the pull request still open on 15 files and none of them reports. The rewrite also took the working-tree copies with it, since a file tracked in the old head and absent from the new one is removed on checkout, and they were restored from the pre-rewrite commit. The review report quotes the user's notes, so that text was public for the interval, and GitHub can still serve the old commits by SHA until it garbage-collects. `reports/` is never `git add -f`'d again.
- `SKILL.md` reached 2,934 tokens against the sibling skill's 1,263, every token of the growth added here, so the measurements moved to `REFERENCE.md`, which is read on demand rather than at activation. A reorder of the two route blocks also truncated the file to 45 lines on the first attempt, and `check.py --update` re-baselined the token table to the wreckage without complaint, which is the argument for asserting on structure before writing rather than trusting a gate that measures size.
- The channel gained a portable route on the user's instruction, and it is the one that needs no account, no token, no record editing, and no page: an `ntfy.sh` topic, published to from a browser, the phone app, or one `curl -d`. `scripts/ntfy_steering.py` ingests a poll body into the same notes and log files the DNS poller writes, deduplicating by ntfy's own message id rather than by a digest of the text, keeping a message's title, skipping `open` and `keepalive`, stripping the markdown fence a page-fetch tool wraps around the body, and falling back to a digest for a message with no id. `STEERING_NTFY_BASELINE=current` holds a topic's existing messages, and the hold is written to the log rather than kept in memory, because a one-shot script that forgets its baseline delivers exactly the history it was told to bury, which the first test run did.
- Three limits of that route were measured rather than assumed, and one of them is a dead end worth recording. The agent cannot publish to ntfy at all: the sandbox's sockets are TLS-closed to it, the page-fetch path is GET-only, and repository secrets answer 403 for the installation token, so a CI relay would have meant committing the topic, and ntfy's own documentation says the topic is essentially a password since anyone who knows it can read and write. So the user's request to prefill a fresh topic cannot be done from here, and the first message has to come from them. An `ntfy.sh` HTTP 500 on the JSON poll form turned out to be the read path rather than the host: the same tool reports HTTP 500 for `httpbin.org/status/200`, a 200 with an empty body, so an empty topic is indistinguishable from an unreachable one, and `/atom` and `/feed.xml` are 404 while the poll form is the only endpoint that terminates instead of streaming forever.
- A verdict the previous entry states was wrong, and the measurement that overturned it came from the user asking a second time. Gists are unreachable from a process in the sandbox, but the agent's own page-fetch tool is a different network path and is not confined the same way: it retrieved a public gist's raw content and a DNS-over-HTTPS answer from `dns.google`, both of which are closed to the sandbox's sockets. That same fetch read the live steering record and showed two directives the poller had not yet surfaced, one asking for a `STEER RECEIVED` acknowledgment clause and one asking that the agent stop agreeing with everything the user says, and both are now clauses in `SKILL.md`, since a channel whose receiving end is invisible to its sender cannot afford a complacent reader.
- The record was never deleted, and the earlier finding that a free zone empties under query volume was half right. The zone served no data for roughly fifty minutes while the provider's panel showed the record present the whole time, and then it recovered on its own, intact. What the poller could not do was tell that apart from its own resolver path going blind, so it printed that the record held no TXT yet, which is a claim about the user's record rather than about the observer. `dns_steering.py` now queries a control name that always holds a TXT record before saying anything about the steering name, and reports which of the two failures it is looking at.
- The sending page lied when it could not reach a resolver, which is the worse kind of bug in a diagnostic. With every DNS-over-HTTPS call failing, the failed samples became the winning value and the light settled on amber `stale`, sending the user to inspect a provider that was fine. It now reports `cannot reach the resolvers` with the reason, and any throw anywhere on the page surfaces in a banner instead of leaving it silently inert, since a page opened inside a sandboxed preview frame cannot touch `localStorage` at all and died on the first keystroke. The record name is pre-filled so the light works before any setup.
- Two questions from the live session were answered by measurement rather than by assumption, and both changed the page. Gists are unreachable three ways, not two: `gist.githubusercontent.com`, which is the host that serves public raw gist content, is TLS-closed like `gist.github.com`, so a gist being public does not help, and `api.github.com/gists` cannot be read anonymously either, because the sandbox's network path injects the installation identity into every request to that host — an invalid `Authorization` header and an empty one both came back with the App's 5,400-request limit and the same 403 `Resource not accessible by integration`. And a browser cannot send to the provider at all: dynv6 answers the preflight with an empty `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, observed in a real browser, and its `GET /api/update` endpoint is the dyndns2 protocol, which carries `ipv4` and `ipv6` and never a TXT value, so there is no CORS-free shortcut either. `assets/steer.html` therefore leads with a terminal command rendered live as the user types, keeps the browser API path for a provider that does send the header, and puts its trust in the DNS-over-HTTPS light, which needs no provider permission at all.
- The rendered command was tested rather than eyeballed, because shell quoting is where this kind of helper usually lies. With a fake `curl` printing its payload, a note carrying an apostrophe, a newline, a double quote, a backslash, an unexpanded `$VAR`, an unexecuted backtick, and a non-ASCII character round-tripped byte-exact through the generated `python3 -c 'json.dumps(sys.argv[1])'`, which is why the note travels as `argv` and not through a pipe or a heredoc: the stdin form appends a newline to every note. An earlier draft also guessed the nsupdate zone by taking the last three labels, which is right for a `dns.army` hostname and wrong for every normal domain, so it uses the zone name the provider reports.
- Verification ran the skill rather than reading it, which the ntfy-only skill never allowed. The checks are `python3 maintenance/check.py --update` plus a clean re-run, `ruff check.`, an empty `ruff format --diff.`, `npx --yes markdownlint-cli2` at 0 issues in its unchanged 8-file scope, `compileall`, and the `validate` workflow on every pushed commit. The DNS transport carries an eighteen-check assert and live demo: the read path against `_dmarc.gmail.com`, an absent name, a multi-string record, and the label guard; the change semantics across first, unchanged, append, clear, and replace; the failure semantics across a failed query, a late baseline in both modes, and unreachable versus empty; the notes and log round trip; the shared log format still parsing for digest recovery; and the startup probe's four cases. What is still unexercised is the write side of the provider's API, which runs on the user's machine and is documented from the provider's specification rather than from a call this sandbox could make.

## 2026-09-15 — Resquash the live rules, fix parity, target nemoe7/wiki, redo init-docs on Diátaxis

- **Summary** — the deferred resquash lands: the Python-project scoping and the project-first commit convention, with the spec-corrected `<type>[optional scope]: <description>` form, the `!` breaking marker, and history-preferred types, are mirrored from `rules/refs/` into `rules/AGENTS.md` and `rules/ARENA.md` in compressed form, and the FORMAT line into `rules/CHATGPT-CUSTOM.txt`. The two parity failures breaking validation — a `Debugging` line in the core and a `When in doubt` line in ARENA that each duplicated a Constitution rule — are removed, and `.github/workflows/distribute-arena.yml` targets `nemoe7/wiki` beside the three existing repos. A follow-up hardens the ChatGPT diagram rule with `NEVER ASCII art` and routes the report-turn closer through the question tool. The deleted `workflows/init-docs.md` returns fully rewritten from the pre-deletion original against the 2026 landscape, and `rules/ARENA.md` prefers read/write tools over shell for file work.
- **Cost** — `rules/AGENTS.md` 1,803 → 1,785 `tok` (-18), `rules/ARENA.md` 9,591 → 9671 `B` (+80), `rules/CHATGPT-CUSTOM.txt` 1,492 → 1,490 `chars` (-2), `rules/CHATGPT-MORE.txt` 1,479 → 1,473 `chars` (-6), `workflows/init-docs.md` new at 4825 `tok` (rewritten from the 4,139 baseline; 429 lines against 911), every addition funded inside the same change: the weaker constitution/body duplicates the redundancy review flagged go (four lines in the core, five in ARENA including a `NEVER mermaid` repeat), plus a micro-trim pass over both files, and the `-f body=@path` war story is cut to its mechanism to fund the file-tools rule. The narrowed ask amendment rides along, refined so the closer goes through the question tool with plain text as fallback only: amended in `rules/refs/ARENA.md` first and mirrored compressed.
- **Deferred** — the `GITHUB` commit-format line in `rules/CHATGPT-CUSTOM.txt` keeps its old wording: the project-first + corrected-format + breaking clause costs ~+55 `chars` against 8 spare, and the 1,500-character field cap is unreachable by trimming, so funding it needs a named rule dropped or a field re-split.

- The parity gate failed on `main`: live `Debugging` held 6 rule lines against 5 in refs (a `MUST grep every caller` repeat of the Constitution line) and live `When in doubt` held 2 against 1 (a stop-and-ask repeat), so both repeats are deleted and each rule survives once.
- The core's Code style now scopes all three Ruff lines to Python projects, uses the project's own `ruff.toml` when it has one, and drops the E4/E7/E9/F codes; its Git section leads with the project's convention first, corrects the Conventional form, and prefers history's types with the spec-mandates-`feat`/`fix` provenance kept. ARENA's Style and Git take the same mirror, with the provenance trimmed to a parenthetical for file size.
- The testing and verification reuse lines now name the project's frameworks, fixtures, helpers, and conventions in both cores, and both verify lines point at the project's tests and the repo's own entrypoints.
- The new ARENA rule sits last in Response in both refs and live: refs carries the full two-sentence form ending in the plain-text fallback, live the one-line `End report turns with an open question via question tool; never mid-task or tool-only.`
- `rules/refs/CHATGPT-MORE.txt` gains `NEVER ASCII art for diagrams` on the mermaid line (refs-first), mirrored as `; NEVER ASCII art` on the live default line; the short-labels/rows merge plus three micro-trims fund it to 1,473 `chars`.
- `nemoe7/wiki` joins `REPOS` in `distribute-arena.yml`; the `ARENA_DIST_PAT` fine-grained PAT needs Contents read/write on it before the next dispatch, like every other target. Access has since been granted.
- `workflows/init-docs.md` is fully rewritten as the universal starting point for repo docs: a tiered catalog (baseline, community bundle, SRS outcome contract, time-axis pair, ADRs and design proposals, Diátaxis quadrants, formal SDD, gated business docs), a no-docs bootstrap, the client choosing every document beyond the baseline, worked classification examples, FAQ dissolution, an unattended-run policy, and no third category between evidence and open questions; the SRS/SDD template enumerations compress to their load-bearing rules the file governs over diataxis.fr on conflict, and `llms.txt` stays out of the catalog as a proposal rather than a standard.
- `rules/refs/ARENA.md` Verification gains the file-tools-over-shell rule with the batching rationale, mirrored into the splice line live; the `-f body=@path` war story is cut to its mechanism to fund it.
- ARENA.md records the harness limits measured 2026-09-11: 100 cheap calls fit one block, the ceiling is result tokens rather than count, verification runs in the next message rather than the same block, and the hard caps are 10 speech clips per turn and image-search `count` <= 5; full findings in refs, two compressed lines live, funded by headroom.
- Root `AGENTS.md` takes the durable-amend and nesting rules: amend-when-durable-and-repo-wide replaces the edit-only-when-asked freeze, and a nearer file wins. They stay out of `rules/refs/AGENTS.md` by decision, so the core keeps its wording and its precedence gap remains open.
- `workflows/init-docs.md` section 10 states the home rule plainly: repo-wide rules and decisions live in the project’s `AGENTS.md`, session scratch stays out.
- Refs and live Use gain the delegation rule: durable repo-wide session rules and decisions belong in the project’s AGENTS.md, amendable unless it says otherwise; thirteen word-level trims fund it with 2 to spare, landing 1,785 `tok`.
- The ARENA harness findings compress to one summary line each in refs and live: 2 CPU workers cap compute-bound shell parallelism, so file work stays on read/write tools; the detailed value lines and the verifier-next-message rule are cut per direction.
- Nesting moves outward: root `AGENTS.md` drops the line as unneeded for this flat repo, and `workflows/init-docs.md` section 10 teaches it to bootstrapped repos instead (nearer wins, closest to the work).
- Squash report, one governing unit per file counted as README counts them: core 1,877 → 1,785 `tok`, ARENA 10,101 → 9671 `B`, ChatGPT-CUSTOM 1,550 → 1,490 `chars` by reverting the unfundable GITHUB line after the mirror proved it over cap, ChatGPT-MORE 1,479 → 1,473 `chars` with the NEVER funded by four trims, `workflows/init-docs.md` new at 4825 `tok` (rewritten from the 4,139 baseline; 429 lines against 911). Not mirrored for size, with refs still holding them: the spec URL in both cores and the `@commitlint/config-conventional` citation in ARENA (kept in the core).

## 2026-09-15 — Drop the planning skill and the init-docs workflow, add the squash baseline, and scope the Python and commit rules to the project

- **Summary** — `skills/planning/` and `workflows/init-docs.md` are gone on request, the first leaving two skills and the second leaving `workflows/` empty but keeping its framework and README, so the directory stays ready for a workflow without shipping one that has no owner. `skills/squash/BASELINE.md` is new: the unsquashed original of that skill's `SKILL.md`, carrying the Agent Skills frontmatter so it is a drop-in entry point once squashed, and documented in `skills/README.md` as the skill-side counterpart of `rules/refs/`. The four refs baselines are amended so the Python tooling rules apply to Python projects rather than to every project, the commit convention defers to the project's own before defaulting to Conventional Commits, and the E4/E7/E9/F codes are dropped, since Ruff's default selection already covers them.
- **Cost** — every amendment lands in `rules/refs/` only. The live mirrors (`rules/AGENTS.md`, `rules/ARENA.md`, `rules/CHATGPT-CUSTOM.txt`) keep their pre-amendment wording on request and stay inside their budgets until a later resquash, so refs and live now disagree on those rules by design and the parity check cannot see it, since it compares headings and rule counts rather than wording. `rules/COMMIT-SPEC.txt` is the exception: it is documented as equal to its baseline and was never compressed, so both copies were rewritten together, which leaves its README budget row stale at 47 `tok` and unmeasurable here, because the sandbox cannot reach `openaipublic.blob.core.windows.net` to download the `cl100k_base` BPE file; the validate workflow's `--update` run refreshes it on push. Two budget rows and their validator entries are deleted with the planning skill and the init-docs workflow, and the README sentence recording init-docs's unfunded 37 `tok` rise goes with the file it described.
- **Deferred** — the four live mirrors still carry the old Python and commit wording; resquashing them is the next pass. The redundancy findings from the same review are recommended but not applied: the constitution-versus-body duplicates in `rules/refs/AGENTS.md`, `rules/refs/ARENA.md`, and root `AGENTS.md`, and the root file's restatement of the installed global core. The precedence gap stays open too: no rule yet says that a nearer project `AGENTS.md` outranks the global core, although agents.md states that the closest file wins. `rules/ARENA.md` was checked for a "commit on a branch other than `main`" rule to omit, per the Arena carve-out, and carries none, so nothing changed there.

- A redundancy review measured the rule corpus first: 698 rule lines across 11 files, compared pairwise on stopword-stripped token sets at a 0.60 Jaccard threshold. Three tiers came out of it — the refs/live mirrors and the deliberate `AGENTS.md`/`ARENA.md` duplication, both by design; the 62 pairs between the two refs baselines, which make one rule change cost four edits; and the unmanaged cluster, where a constitution line repeats a body line, sometimes verbatim and sometimes in a weaker form that drops the MUST.
- The Python amendments split one rule into four in `rules/refs/AGENTS.md`, so the Ruff selection, the `ruff.toml` conventions, the create-it-if-missing rule, and the pre-commit gates each open with "For a Python project". `rules/refs/ARENA.md` takes the same scoping on its four Ruff lines, and `rules/refs/CHATGPT-CUSTOM.txt` restates its FORMAT line as "Python projects: use Ruff with its default rule selection". The `ruff.toml` conventions are now described as the project's own file when it has one, so a repository with its existing config is no longer overwritten.
- The testing and verification rules take the same treatment: "Verify behavior with the project's own tests, linters, formatters, and builds", and the reuse rules now name the project's frameworks, fixtures, helpers, and conventions. The discipline stays global; only the thing it points at became project-scoped.
- The commit amendment adds one rule ahead of the format: follow the project's commit-message convention when the project states one. The format itself is kept in full and corrected against the specification at <https://www.conventionalcommits.org/en/v1.0.0/>, which mandates `<type>[optional scope]: <description>` with a scope in parentheses and `!` before the colon for a breaking change, rather than the `<type>(scope): <subject>` form these files carried. The type list stays, with the provenance now stated: the specification mandates only `feat` and `fix`, and the rest come from the Angular convention through `@commitlint/config-conventional`, so the types the project's history already uses win.
- `workflows/README.md` loses both of its tables and states in prose that the directory currently holds no workflows, while its Format section still defines what a new workflow must meet. Root `AGENTS.md` keeps its Workflows section and its read-first link, since the framework it points at still exists.
- `skills/squash/BASELINE.md` restates the whole skill in complete sentences: the unit table and its five-step resolution order, the rule against silently substituting a proxy unit, mixed budgets as simultaneous constraints with the conflict reported rather than resolved, the seven categories that must survive, the six that are safe to cut, the six-step method with the small-passes rationale, the four constraints on structure, register, verbatim spans, and voice, the rule that new content must be funded in the same change, and the reporting contract. It closes with a "When in doubt" section, matching the shape the rules baselines use.
- The install example in `skills/README.md` now copies `squash` instead of `planning`, and the Format section gains a bullet defining `BASELINE.md` and its amend-then-squash order.

## 2026-09-13 — Add automations/, fit the daily prompt to its ChatGPT runtime, and enforce how ARENA.md loads

- **Summary** — a new top-level `automations/` directory holds `DAILIES.md`, the recurring combined daily monitoring prompt, rewritten to the `rules/refs/GUIDELINES.md` standard as a self-contained file and then refitted for the runtime that actually executes it, a ChatGPT scheduled monitoring task; the same pull request closes the gap that left `ARENA.md` unenforced, with an in-file rule requiring an Arena agent to open it before its first edit, a validator gate on the root copy, the corrected loading premise and a recorded bootstrap line in the specification, and post-sync verification in the distribution workflow.
- **Cost** — `rules/ARENA.md` 9,841 → 9,716 `B` (-125): the activation rule costs 139 `B` and two compression passes recover 145 `B`, then the ask-discipline amendments cost about 50 `B` more and a third pass recovers about 175 `B`, so the file lands 125 `B` under the baseline README had recorded as an accepted rise, while `rules/refs/ARENA.md` takes the full wording at 13,264 → 13,591 `B` (+327). `rules/AGENTS.md` 1,818 → 1,816 `tok` and `rules/CHATGPT-CUSTOM.txt` 1,351 → 1,492 `chars`, leaving 8 of its 1,500 spare, carry the same amendments funded inside the same change, and README's recorded-exception note now says both files were squashed back below their accepted baselines, so item 5 applies to them again. `rules/CHATGPT-MORE.txt` 305 → 803 `chars` takes the deferred debt, still 697 spare. `automations/DAILIES.md` stays untracked by request, with no budget row and no squash pass: 124 lines and 1,649 `tok` (`cl100k_base`) at 7,498 `B` on arrival, 163 lines and 2,928 `tok` at 12,780 `B` after the runtime refit, 176 lines and 3,213 `tok` at 14,101 `B` once the sources were confirmed, the onboarding rules landed, and the stamp moved to the end of the run, and 177 lines and 3,302 `tok` at 14,471 `B` once the two store listings gave way to their official version channels and the unattended carve-out replaced its `stop and ask` rule, its 71 rule lines becoming 111, and 3,304 `tok` at 14,482 `B` once its timestamp line was relabeled to name what it holds, then 178 lines, 112 rule lines, and 3,366 `tok` at 14,768 `B` once the runtime facts were corrected, the report took the ASD-STE100 rule, and the connector's write access was recorded.
- **Deferred** — `automations/` has no directory README, `maintenance/check.py` does not validate `DAILIES.md`, and the root `AGENTS.md` still carries no read-first link or Automations section for it. The distribution workflow stays dispatch-only: a schedule was considered and skipped, so a repository created between dispatches receives `ARENA.md` only once someone adds it to `REPOS` and runs the workflow. Headroom, Bambuddy, and OpenGym are confirmed and their `unresolved:` marker is gone. The last open item from the runtime critique is decided: the two store listings gave way to their official version channels, so the disputed `version history` wording is gone rather than reworded. The ask-discipline set was chosen on request — prefer the question tool, ask before implementing, never block an unattended run — and the rejected broader alternative is recorded below. The embedded-datetime MUST stays, by design rather than by default, with its timing amended on request — see the two state bullets below.

- The prompt is restructured from run-on prose into rules without losing one: a `## Use` preamble declaring what the file is, that it applies to every run, that it outranks skills and plugins while an explicit instruction in the run prompt outranks it, and that sections 1 to 4 feed the report section 5 shapes; a nine-rule `## Constitution` holding the run-level rules that must survive compaction; the five numbered sections; and a closing `## When in doubt`.
- The Constitution carries the embedded current UTC+8 (Asia/Manila) datetime `2026-09-12 17:40:00` beside its two rules — update it on EVERY RUN before monitoring from the actual execution time, and leave it alone when the prompt is merely edited or viewed — plus actionable-only output, the NEVER on no-change and informational-only entries, first-run baselines that are not reported, ephemeral items kept until their purpose is complete or they are explicitly removed, the exact section 5 contract, and output-nothing when no section is actionable.
- Section 1's 11 monitored items and their preferred sources move into a two-column table so each binds to exactly one source: `MacroDroid` and `Idle Obelisk Miner` share the Google Play version history, `Tailscale` uses its changelog, `Arena.ai` its product changelog, and `OpenGym` GitLab releases, with the rest on GitHub releases. No source URL is embedded, because the report's `Source` cell must carry the direct official link found at run time.
- Section 3's 30 rules keep their original order under four `###` groups — Release baseline, Release recommendation, Findings table, Branch audit — so the top-level numbering the report depends on stays 1 to 5. The commit-history-first rule stays a MUST with the ancestor release/tag baseline, individual commits and diffs stay the primary basis with aggregates only verifying, the aggregate-file-count prohibition stays a NEVER, and the private-repo rule keeps its split between recommending public plus wiki and NEVER making it public.
- Section 4 keeps the two LiteLLM identifiers distinct: merged PR #39157 is tracked by its final PR head commit `9e7cfee8d7cba55dbf11b61d2d2fdce94c70037e`, with merge commit `59da6e75a50024dcca1af5efa90e4eec340b89b` referenceable but explicitly not the tracked identifier, and notification gated on an official stable release rather than a dev or pre-release inclusion.
- A `simplified:` marker in `## Use` states the ceiling on purpose: the run covers only the 11 listed software items and the `nemoe7` repositories, and monitoring anything else means editing that section.
- `automations/DAILIES.md` is self-contained on request: a run needs no other file, so `## Use` states that and the file cites nothing, carrying no cross-reference to `rules/refs/GUIDELINES.md` as meta. It keeps the shape those guidelines prescribe — Use, Constitution, numbered domains, When in doubt — without depending on them at run time.
- `rules/ARENA.md` and `rules/refs/ARENA.md` gain the activation rule in Use, amended in refs first: no platform loads the file, so an agent that was not given it in context MUST open it at the repository root before its first edit and confirm in one line that it is loaded. The live mirror compresses to `No platform loads this file: if it is not in your context, MUST open it at the repo root before your first edit and confirm in one line.`
- The rise is funded in the same change, per README item 5, by two passes that remove words and never rules. Pass one merges the snapshot-limit pair into one line, drops `, etc.` from the cache-directory examples, merges the question-count and restate-the-count pair, folds `Not needed during execution` into the final-response rule, and drops the strikethrough rationale. Pass two drops three clauses the refs keep in full: `Arena's controlled edits make pushing safe`, `which Arena cannot render`, and `a 200 is not proof`. 9,841 → 9,926 → 9,835 `B`, and root `ARENA.md` is re-copied byte-identical.
- `maintenance/check.py` now gates that identity instead of leaving it to a manual `cmp`: a `ROOT_COPIES` check compares the root `ARENA.md` bytes with `rules/ARENA.md` and reports a drifted or missing copy together with the `cp` command that fixes it, and the pass message names the new check. Root `AGENTS.md` folds its verification step 4 into step 3 and lists the check under Repository type; `maintenance/README.md` describes it; and the Arena file section of `rules/README.md` replaces its `rather than gating the identity` corner cut with the gate.
- `rules/README.md` corrects the premise the stand-alone design rested on. It claimed `Arena loads ARENA.md on its own, without AGENTS.md`; the recorded observation, with its date and repository, is that an Arena session's injected context carries the sandbox, branch, and tool details only, and the agent reaches the file by opening it. That also ends the contradiction with the same file's `never assume a filename alone enables loading`. The contents row and the Arena file section now carry the exact bootstrap line for a session's first message or the platform's custom-instructions field, and state that delivery is not activation. That section is split into three paragraphs — condensing, activation, and the root copy — because one paragraph carried all three.
- Root `AGENTS.md` aligns its Arena pointer with the deployed core: `also follow` becomes `MUST also read and follow... before your first edit`. The file is unbudgeted, so the alignment is free, and its two bold clauses stay untouched at the emphasis cap.
- `.github/workflows/distribute-arena.yml` verifies its own work: it resolves the source once as an absolute path, fails before the loop when `rules/ARENA.md` is missing from the checkout, and after each target's commit and push compares `git show HEAD:$DEST` against the source, failing that repository loudly into the existing `sync failed for:` error list. The header comment records that the workflow is dispatch-only, that a repository not in `REPOS` never receives the file, and that delivery is not activation.
- A critique pass against the real runtime preceded the refit: the prompt had been aligned to guidelines written for a file-based coding agent, so it demanded state a scheduled task does not keep, tools it does not have, and two sources that do not publish what the prompt expected. Findings 1, 6, 7, 8, 9, 11, and 12 were approved and are amended here; 4 and 5 were answered with facts (the GitHub connector exists, and the source URLs are embedded); 13 and 14 were rejected, so the file stays self-contained and stays one combined run; 2 is left as written with recommendations only; 3 is disputed and recorded below with its evidence rather than changed.
- State is now anchored to the automation metadata instead of memory, which no scheduled run carries: a new `## Run state and evidence` section reads `last_run`, `next_run`, `updated`, `timezone`, `schedule`, `enabled`, `paused_count`, and `completed_count`, defines the comparison window as `last_run` to the current run time, widens it when `last_run` is older than `schedule` or `paused_count` rose, falls back to the 24 hours before the run when the metadata is not visible, and takes every printed date from the metadata rather than from recall. `## 1` and `## 3` now say `inside the comparison window` where they said `since the previous check`.
- The Constitution gains the two rules the critique found missing: the metadata wins over the embedded datetime when both are available, and NEVER state a version, tag, date, or SHA that is not in content fetched this run, NEVER claim a check you did not run. The embedded-datetime rules stay verbatim, since a run cannot edit its own prompt; the precedence line resolves the collision instead of deleting either rule.
- Coverage becomes the one sanctioned exception to actionable-only: `## Coverage` is appended after `## 4. Watched` as the only permitted addition to the exact section list, and carries the sources and repositories reached, the ones missed with the reason, and the sections left incomplete. Unreachable sources are actionable, so the rule that suppresses informational entries no longer suppresses the report's own gaps. A stop rule goes with it: work the sections in the order 1, 4, 2, 3 so the expensive audit runs last, and output what completed rather than filling a gap with inferred content.
- Section 1's eleven items now carry their source URLs, each verified before it was written: the five GitHub releases pages through the GitHub API, `https://tailscale.com/changelog` and both Google Play listings by fetching them live, and `https://arena.ai/company/product-changelog` from its own page. `https://gitlab.com/DuarteSantos8/opengym/-/releases` is built from the project path that release mirrors report, because `gitlab.com` is unreachable from the sandbox, and is the one URL here not fetched directly. Headroom stays `unresolved:` with its three candidate repositories listed inline, since guessing would put a fabricated source in a monitoring prompt; the run skips it and names it in Coverage.
- The two Google Play rows keep the wording they had, and the run is told to report only what a listing exposes and never to substitute a third-party tracker in the Source cell. The fetched evidence: MacroDroid's listing shows `Updated on Sep 8, 2026` with no version number and no history, while Idle Obelisk Miner's shows `Updated on` plus a What's new block whose first line carries `v2.2.15`, so the official observable differs per app and full version history exists only on third-party trackers.
- Section 5 defines what it previously left to the model: the empty-run output on a surface that cannot send nothing, a legend fixing 🔴 High to a blocking or secret-exposing finding with 🟠 Medium and 🟡 Low as the prompt already assigned them, and a one-line meaning for `Ver.`, `Source`, `Evidence`, `Docs`, `Section`, `Why`, `Latest checked version`, and a closed `Status` enum. Its four example rows use `example.invalid` and `v0.0.0` placeholders on purpose: a file a model copies from must not contain a value it could mistake for a finding.
- Section 4 gains its completion path, which the ephemeral-item rule assumed but never defined: a material change is a stable release containing the tracked commit, a revert, or an official statement that it will not ship, and once that is reported the item is complete, stops being checked, and asks in one line to be removed from the prompt. Section 3 gains the honesty valve that makes its git-shaped MUSTs survivable without a shell: where the connector cannot prove ancestry, CI state, or pull-request state, the Evidence cell says so instead of inferring it. Section 2's scope is named as `nemoe7`'s public repositories rather than GitHub at large, and capped at five recommendations per run.
- The state design was then corrected on the owner's evidence, which reverses part of the refit: a run can sometimes edit this prompt, one run's timestamp update landed and another's did not, and the automation metadata can itself be unreliable. The embedded datetime is therefore the running record of the last completed run rather than a fallback, the comparison window starts at the later of it and `last_run`, a disagreement between the two is named in Coverage, an update that does not land is reported in Coverage with `last_run` treated as the record of that run, and a run with no visible metadata falls back to the embedded datetime alone instead of to a bare 24-hour window. The three recommended replacements for that MUST are moot; the field stays.
- The stamp's timing was then amended on request, which closes the flaw the unreliable edit channel opened: `before monitoring` becomes `after the report is written, using this run's actual start time`, so a run that dies part-way leaves the previous stamp in place and the next run re-covers that window instead of swallowing it, and the embedded value now means what the Constitution already called it — the start time of the last run that completed. The trade is stated in the prompt rather than left to the model: a re-covered window may repeat an item already reported, and reporting it again costs less than a missed release.
- Section 1's sources are confirmed: Headroom is `headroomlabs-ai/headroom`, the token-compression library, proxy, and MCP server, and `maziggy/bambuddy` and `DuarteSantos8/opengym` are the right projects, so the `unresolved:` row and its skip rule are gone and all eleven items now carry one fetched-or-mirrored URL each.
- Section 1 gains `### Tracking a new item`, eight embedded rules for onboarding a tracked software item without a maintainer having to reverse-engineer the table: add one row holding the name and a source URL fetched to prove it exists, prefer GitHub or GitLab releases and fall back to the official changelog, store listing, or vendor feed only when the project publishes none, record the version or date the URL showed so the first run has a baseline rather than a gap, define what counts as stable when a channel mixes stable releases with pre-releases, mark a temporary item ephemeral so it is removed when its purpose is complete, keep section 3's scope to `nemoe7` repositories, keep the `simplified:` item count in `## Use` equal to the table's rows, and remove a row only on an explicit instruction.
- Both store listings were then replaced by official version channels that the fetched evidence showed exist. `https://idleobeliskminer.com/patchnotes` publishes Idle Obelisk Miner release by release — v2.2.21 and v2.2.20 in September 2026, v2.2.15 and v2.2.14 on 31 August, each with full notes and its own permalink — and MacroDroid's developer forum board `macrodroid-news-and-announcements.3` is where ArloSoft posts its stable `[UPDATE] 5.x` threads, with the Play listing itself linking that forum as the official channel. The `version history` wording is therefore gone rather than reworded: the intent was right and the store was the wrong place to look.
- Two traps are encoded with the swap. MacroDroid's forum also runs a `Beta Releases` board where the developer posts betas because, in its own words, Play Store review times have gotten ridiculous, so section 1 names the announcements board as the only stable source and ignores the beta board explicitly instead of leaving the pre-release rule to find it. And Play's `Updated on` is not a stable identifier — the same Idle Obelisk listing showed `Sep 11, 2026` at `?hl=en` and `31 Aug 2026` at `?hl=en-SG` in one search pass — so a listing pinned to `&hl=en` may corroborate a date but never appears in the Source cell, and `### Tracking a new item` now calls a store listing date corroboration rather than a version source.
- The rule files gain the ask discipline voted for, amended in refs first and mirrored compressed. Arena's Verification prefers the question tool for every question instead of only falling back to plain text when the tool fails, and names the mechanism once globally rather than in three separate lines, which is what funded the round; both cores' Scope rules require the ask before implementing rather than after; and the generic core plus `rules/CHATGPT-CUSTOM.txt`, where scheduled tasks actually run, carry the carve-out that an unattended run is never blocked on a question but records it and proceeds on a stated assumption. That carve-out is deliberately absent from `rules/ARENA.md`: `AGENTS.md` stays in force in Arena, so the core carries it, and Arena has no unattended surface.
- The broader alternative was rejected on the record. A rule to stop and ask before every edit, command, and commit would contradict the materiality threshold and the assume-and-state branch sitting beside it, deadlock the unattended runs this repository now ships, turn a one-line fix into a two-turn exchange, and wear out the signal, since a week of approving routine asks gets the material ones waved through. The failure actually observed here is an agent improvising past material ambiguity, which the timing clause addresses directly.
- The automation itself then had to obey the carve-out, which its own last rule contradicted: `## When in doubt` ended with `stop and ask rather than improvise an entry`, a deadlock in a run with nobody attached. It now records the question in `## Coverage`, proceeds on the most reasonable assumption, and states it rather than improvising an entry, and section 5's Coverage definition — a closed list, which the Constitution requires the report to follow exactly — gains the matching item, `any question you could not ask with the assumption you proceeded on`, so the deferred question reaches the owner as a report line instead of stalling the run.
- The tool preference was then generalized on request, from an Arena rule into a core one: `rules/AGENTS.md` and its ref now prefer the question tool whenever the surface provides one, so any agent with such a tool reaches for it instead of asking the same questions in prose. The wording is conditional on purpose — "when the surface has one" — because the surfaces differ: Arena keeps its own copy of the preference, since its activation rule exists precisely because `AGENTS.md` may not be loaded there; `rules/CLINE.md` needs no change, inheriting the core rule while still naming its concrete `ask_question`, and it has zero token headroom; and `rules/CHATGPT-CUSTOM.txt` is untouched, because ChatGPT offers no such tool and a conditional rule costs it nothing. A third compression pass funded the addition, landing the core at 1,816 `tok`, 2 under its baseline.
- The ChatGPT field mapping was then corrected on the owner's account, superseding a claim this entry made earlier. Personalization no longer offers the two boxes the files were documented against — what ChatGPT should know, and how it should respond — but four fields: `Custom Instructions`, `Nickname`, `Occupation`, and `More about you`, the first and last capped at 1,500 characters. `rules/CHATGPT-CUSTOM.txt` maps to `Custom Instructions` and `rules/CHATGPT-MORE.txt` to `More about you`, so no merge is needed and the 1,500-character gate in `maintenance/check.py` was already correct; what was wrong was the naming, in `rules/README.md`'s contents table and rationale and in the validator's comment. The `rules/refs/` copies remain uncompressed baselines, never pasted anywhere, and `Nickname` and `Occupation` are structured profile lines rather than rule space, deliberately not versioned.
- Whether `More about you` can hold rules at all was settled by experiment rather than inference, after a leaked 2024 system-prompt wrapper was found telling the model that profile content is not relevant to 99% of requests and to acknowledge it only when a request is directly related. Two canaries were placed in the field and run in three fresh chats: an unconditional rule (always begin with a marker word) fired on all three, including a request on a wholly unrelated topic, while a conditional obligation (flag a reply needing more than two steps) fired on the topically related request and was dropped on the unrelated one whose four-point answer plainly met its trigger. The field therefore binds unconditional instructions across topics and deweights judgment-triggered ones when the request is unrelated, which is what the leaked wrapper describes. The first two canaries left the coding case uninformative — field one's no-unnecessary-prose rule left that answer with no prose for the banned word to appear in, and a one-block answer is arguably not multi-step — so a third canary tested it directly: a conditional work rule, mark any reply containing code, fired on a coding request. Conditional work rules therefore bind in that field where they are needed, and the deweighting on unrelated requests is harmless, since a work rule is only needed on a work request.
- That result paid a debt the 2026-09-13 ponytail and frontend-design entry had deferred for want of room: the seven-rung ladder and the three MUST upgrades — propose a smaller scope, leave one runnable check, grep every caller before editing. The rungs go into `Custom Instructions` in place of field one's four-step ladder, so no two fields carry competing versions of it, funded by trims in the same change and landing at 1,492 of 1,500 characters. The three MUSTs and the ladder's reflex caveat go into `More about you`, at 803 of 1,500, under a `WORK:` inline label beside the existing `RESPONSE:` block. The caveat names the field the rungs live in rather than saying above, because the two halves are pasted into different boxes and a dangling reference would be worse than a long one. Both refs baselines take the full uncompressed wording first, per the amendment order.
- `maintenance/check.py`'s refs/live parity gate caught a placement error in this round: the new unattended-run rule landed in the live Constitution while its refs counterpart sits in Scope, and a live file may merge lines but never add rules. The rule was relocated to Scope, matching refs, and the gate passed.
- The two ChatGPT files were then renamed on request to name the field each is pasted into rather than the content they started with: `rules/CHATGPT.txt` becomes `rules/CHATGPT-CUSTOM.txt` for `Custom Instructions`, and `rules/CHATGPT_RESPONSE.txt` becomes `rules/CHATGPT-MORE.txt` for `More about you`, which matters because the second file no longer holds only response rules. Both refs baselines were renamed with them, and the eight referencing places followed: `EXPECTED_BUDGETS`, `PLAIN_PAIRS`, and `CHATGPT_FIELDS` in `maintenance/check.py`, the budgets table in `README.md`, the overlay list in the root `AGENTS.md`, three places in `rules/README.md` including its commit-discipline table, and `rules/refs/README.md`, whose two lines still described the dead two-box layout as the first and second custom-instruction fields. The older entries in this file keep the names they were accurate under.
- Ten more core rules then moved into `rules/CHATGPT-MORE.txt` on request, once the canaries had shown that field binds work rules on work requests: an explicit chat instruction outranks these rules, open with the result, the full debug cycle — reproduce, isolate, hypothesize, verify, fix root cause not symptom, cover, recheck — never lazy about understanding, build the full version without re-arguing when the user insists, follow repo docs and conventions, prefer deletion over addition and boring over clever, check requirements and scope and acceptance criteria before finishing, report skipped alternatives with add-when triggers, and mark a corner-cut with a `simplified:` comment. The precedence rule sits above both blocks because it governs them, the two response rules join `RESPONSE:`, and the rest join `WORK:`, each compressed as usual against the refs baseline that takes the full wording first. That fills the field from 803 to 1,484 of its 1,500 characters and its refs baseline from 1,289 to 2,200 `B`. Left out as poor value per character: the plan MUSTs at about 200 characters, which only pay off when plans are requested in chat; the `ruff.toml` specifics and gates-before-commit, which are repo-bound; review-the-diff, which has no diff to act on; cohesive modules, seams, and simplest-implementation, which the ladder already carries; and the merge and scope-reuse rules, which chat rarely reaches. Both fields now hold 24 spare characters between them, so the next rule added has to be funded by compression or by dropping one — the defer-and-pay cycle this entry has been running is no longer cheap.
- Three standing nits closed in the same round. `rules/AGENTS.md` capitalizes `NEVER block an unattended run`, matching its refs counterpart and the file's own convention of reserving caps for irreversible, dangerous, and honesty rules: a stalled automation is a silent failure, and the file's other sentence-case `Never` is a persona line, so the caps now tell the two apart. It measured at zero tokens, 1,816 before and after, so no budget row moved. The root `AGENTS.md` gains an `## Automations` section where it already had `## Rules`, `## Skills`, and `## Workflows`, saying that automation prompts are self-contained text pasted into an external scheduler rather than rules an agent loads, that `DAILIES.md` runs on web search and the GitHub connector with no shell and no repo access, that the prompts carry no budget row and no gate because the scheduler owns its own copy, and that an edit in the repo never reaches a running automation. And `automations/DAILIES.md` relabels its state line from `Current UTC+8 (Asia/Manila) datetime` to `Last completed run started (UTC+8, Asia/Manila)`, which is what the surrounding rules already said the value meant; the old label invited a human or a self-editing run to correct it to now and destroy the coverage window. 177 lines and 111 rule lines unchanged, 3,304 `tok` at 14,482 `B`.
- Two automation items stay deferred by choice rather than by cost: `automations/README.md`, worth writing once a second automation exists, since `DAILIES.md`'s own `## Use` section covers today's need; and a narrow validator gate over `automations/DAILIES.md` — the `simplified:` count against the section-1 row count, the section-5 name list, one source URL per row, and a parseable embedded datetime — which is the highest-value item still open precisely because the file's editor is an unreliable automation. The `distribute-arena.yml` schedule stays dispatch-only: a cron pusher would carry a broken `rules/ARENA.md` to every target repository, and compare-before-push gives idempotency rather than correctness. The owner confirmed dispatch-only on 2026-09-13, so that one is a decision rather than a deferral and is no longer an open item.
- The runtime facts were then corrected on the owner's account, superseding what `automations/DAILIES.md` and the root `AGENTS.md` both claimed. The task runs unattended at most once a day, and also whenever the user asks for it; the GitHub connector is linked to the account this repository lives on; and a terminal with local files is available when the task itself decides it needs one, so a prompt must not depend on that but is not forbidden it either. The earlier text said there is no shell and no git, which was wrong, and no repo access, which was too absolute. Reports still go to the chat response, and the question of persisting them was settled below.
- `rules/CHATGPT-MORE.txt` now says Mermaid diagrams rather than Mermaid alone, on the owner's concern that the bare name may not be recognized: `Default to Mermaid diagrams for pipelines/flows`, funded by dropping two articles from the understanding rule so the file landed at 1,480 characters rather than rising. Its refs baseline carries the explanation the compressed field cannot — a fenced code block tagged mermaid that a supporting surface renders as a picture — and says to name it as Mermaid diagrams. The core's own line still reads `Default to mermaid for pipelines, diagrams, and flows`; the owner scoped this amendment to the ChatGPT field, so the core is recommended, not changed.
- `rules/COMMIT_SPEC.txt` became `rules/COMMIT-SPEC.txt` with its refs baseline renamed in lockstep, because the hyphen is the repository's only other multiword separator, in `workflows/init-docs.md`, and moving one pair costs less than moving the two ChatGPT files back to underscores. Seven referencing places across five files followed: `EXPECTED_BUDGETS` and `PLAIN_PAIRS` in `maintenance/check.py`, the budgets table, three places in `rules/README.md` including the one-rule-per-line note, and `rules/refs/README.md`. The older entries in this file keep the underscore they were accurate under.
- Every rule file that produces human-facing text now prefers ASD-STE100 Simplified Technical English, on request, as one line with no linter, spec file, or gate behind it: the model decides whether to follow the standard and looks it up when it needs the rules, and the refs baselines carry that full wording. `rules/AGENTS.md` takes it as a new `General` line naming responses, comments, and docs; `rules/ARENA.md` folds it into its existing `General` style line, because a standalone line would not fit; `rules/CHATGPT-MORE.txt` takes it as a second global line above the `RESPONSE:` and `WORK:` blocks; and `automations/DAILIES.md` takes it as a report rule, since that prompt must stand alone and its report is the most-read human-facing text here. Both budgeted markdown files funded it inside the same change, as compression item 5 requires: `rules/AGENTS.md` dropped an exact duplicate of its keep-behavior line, which the Constitution digest already carries, and merged the two Python lines that both restated the Ruff defaults, landing at 1,812 `tok` from 1,816; `rules/ARENA.md` funded itself with nine word-level trims that keep every negation, number, and command, landing at 9,710 `B` from 9,716. `rules/CHATGPT-MORE.txt` funded its line with four telegraphic trims and landed unchanged at 1,480 of 1,500 characters. Two files deliberately did not take the line: `rules/CLINE.md` states that generic rules are not repeated there and loads alongside `AGENTS.md`, which now carries it, and `rules/COMMIT-SPEC.txt` is a format reference whose 47 `tok` would nearly double for a prose-style rule.
- The remaining automation questions were then settled on the owner's account rather than left open. The GitHub connector can read and write in this repository, and no rule amendment followed, because pushes, branches, and pull requests are already gated by the git rules in `rules/CHATGPT-CUSTOM.txt`, which that account loads on every request including a scheduled one; only the documentation changed, in the root `AGENTS.md` and in the runtime line of `automations/DAILIES.md`. Reports stay in the chat response and nothing is persisted. The validator gate was declined once its four candidate checks and their cost were laid out — the timestamp format, the `simplified:` count against the section-1 rows, the section-5 name list, and one source URL per row, roughly 70 to 95 lines with the self-check the runnable-check MUST would require — so `automations/` stays ungated and unbudgeted. Its rationale needed correcting on the way there: a gate would watch the repository copy, which is where a re-paste comes from, and could not see the live copy inside ChatGPT, so the unreliable editor it was meant to police edits its own text and never this file. The three exclusions from this round's amendments also stand as written: `rules/CLINE.md` does not repeat generic rules, `rules/COMMIT-SPEC.txt` is a format reference, and the core's mermaid line keeps its lower-case wording. `automations/README.md` is now the only deferred item, and `distribute-arena.yml` stays dispatch-only by decision.
- Handed-off commands now get printed in two forms, on request: bash, and Windows as PowerShell by default or cmd for a single line, because PowerShell handles multiline better. The trigger is a command the agent hands the user to run rather than one it ran itself, and setup, install, and multi-step commands always get both forms; the owner keeps an always-on Raspberry Pi for the bash side, and that reason lives in the refs baselines where uncompressed wording goes. All three refs took the full rule first. `rules/AGENTS.md` carries it in `Responses` and landed at 1,811 `tok` from 1,812, funded by widening the Constitution's invent line to cover a requirement — which the Engineering restatement it replaced already did — dropping that restatement, and three word-level trims. `rules/ARENA.md` carries a tighter form of the same rule in `Response` and landed at 9,698 `B` from 9,710, funded by dropping a Verification line the Constitution already states and by seven more trims, the last of which drops `for approval` from the smaller-scope line while `propose before implementing` keeps the ask-first sense. `rules/CHATGPT-MORE.txt` could not carry the rule with both its conditions intact on 20 spare characters, because compression item 2 forbids treating a condition as removable, so the owner chose the trade: the `simplified:` corner-cut line came out, freeing 73 characters, the command rule went in at 88, and three telegraphic trims landed the field at 1,479 of 1,500. That corner-cut rule is not lost — it stays in `rules/AGENTS.md`, `rules/ARENA.md`, and all three refs — but ChatGPT no longer carries it, which makes it the only rule this pull request has taken out of a live file to make room for another.
- Checks run: `maintenance/check.py --update` passes (3 skills, 1 workflow, README measurements, the markdownlint scope, refs/live parity, and the new root-copy gate); the eleven source URLs were verified as described above rather than assumed; `npx --yes markdownlint-cli2` reports 0 issues across the same 8 linted files, and an ad-hoc pass over `automations/DAILIES.md` under the same config is clean at 0 issues, which is what turned section 3's four bold run-in labels into `###` headings (MD036) and put the eleven source URLs in code spans (MD034), staying at 0 after the onboarding subsection and the state rules were added; a splice that dropped the Google Play rule inside the new subsection was caught on review and moved back above it; `ruff check.`, `ruff format --diff.`, and `python -m compileall maintenance/check.py` are clean at the pinned 0.16.6. Both new checks were exercised red before green: a drifted root copy and a missing one each fail with their own message, and a diverged distribution target fails the comparison in a scratch repository, while the identical pairs pass. The workflow YAML parses. `tiktoken`'s `cl100k_base` host is unreachable from the sandbox, so the cache was seeded from the `niieani/gpt-tokenizer` mirror through the GitHub contents API as `maintenance/README.md` documents.

## 2026-09-13 — Drop ponytail and frontend-design, rebuild the simplicity rules from the ladder up

- **Summary** — both adapted skills are deleted with every reference to them; the rules keep the 2026-09-09 ponytail-lite set, regain all six 2026-09-11 parts at the intensity voted for, and carry the full seven-rung ladder for the first time; `rules/ARENA.md` also gains a client-unreliability clause.
- **Cost** — `rules/AGENTS.md` 1,731 → 1,818 `tok` (+87), `rules/ARENA.md` 9,205 → 9,841 `B` (+636), `skills/web-interface-guidelines/SKILL.md` 533 → 531 `tok`; two budget rows retire with their skills (2,011 `tok` and 1,675 `tok`). The removals first took AGENTS to 1,601 `tok` and ARENA to 8,861 `B`, so the ladder and the three MUST upgrades are what carry the rise, accepted on request rather than squashed and recorded in `README.md` as the files' new baseline.
- **Deferred** — `rules/CHATGPT.txt` stays untouched: it held no ultra content to remove, and the new MUST rules need roughly 200 of its 149 spare characters. Add them when the field frees up, or drop its mermaid clause to make room.

- Per-part decisions, applied to `rules/refs/` first and then mirrored compressed into the live files. Scope-challenge returns as a MUST: propose a smaller scope for approval before implementing when the brief looks bigger than the need, with the how-never-what guardrail kept (simplicity chooses how to meet the brief, NEVER what to silently drop). Shaping heuristics and decision heuristics return whole, as `Prefer` lines. One-check test minimalism returns as a MUST: non-trivial logic — a branch, a loop, a parser, a money or security path — leaves one runnable check behind, an assert-based demo or a single small test file, with no new frameworks or fixtures unless asked. The grep-callers root-cause rule returns as the hard rule it was, with its `Fix once where all callers route through` clause and its Constitution echo. Skipped/add-when reporting returns soft, as `Consider`, in the Responses sections of both files.
- The ladder is new to the rule files. `rules/refs/AGENTS.md` and `rules/refs/ARENA.md` carry all seven rungs in full upstream wording — needed at all, already in this codebase, stdlib, native platform feature, installed dependency, one line, minimum code — plus the reflex-not-a-research-project caveat and its two-rungs-take-the-higher-one tie-break. The live files compress that to one enumerated rung list and one caveat line. Rung 4's upstream example is written as prose rather than markup because MD033 (no inline HTML) is on in the markdownlint defaults that cover `rules/`.
- `rules/ARENA.md` and its ref gain a client-unreliability clause beside the existing duplicated/garbled-message rule in Verification: the client resends messages, truncates or drops replies, and returns empty results from tools that did run, so a repeated or identical message is a resend rather than a new instruction — answer whatever is still pending, restate what is already done in one line, and never take a resend as authorization to redo finished work or to widen scope. Root `ARENA.md` is re-copied and stays byte-identical.
- `rules/CHATGPT.txt` and `rules/refs/CHATGPT.txt` are unchanged: they held no ultra content, verified by grepping both for caller, skipped, deletion over, boring, fewest files, runnable check, simplest implementation, edge-case, new framework, and smaller scope with zero matches.
- `skills/ponytail/` and `skills/frontend-design/` are deleted, and their references go with them: three tables in `skills/README.md` (contents, upstream sources, selection), the root `README.md` skills line and its two budget rows, the two `EXPECTED_BUDGETS` entries in `maintenance/check.py`, and `skills/web-interface-guidelines/SKILL.md`'s pointer, which now reads "in place of design work" rather than naming a skill that no longer exists. `web-interface-guidelines` is the only remaining adapted skill. Historical CHANGELOG mentions of both skills stay: they are the record of what was incorporated, when, and at what cost.
- Checks run: `maintenance/check.py --update` passes (3 skills, 1 workflow, README measurements, the markdownlint scope, refs/live parity); markdownlint-cli2 reports 0 issues across the 8 linted files; `ruff check` and `ruff format --diff` are clean at the pinned 0.16.6; `cmp rules/ARENA.md ARENA.md` is identical. `tiktoken`'s `cl100k_base` host and `raw.githubusercontent.com` are unreachable from the sandbox, so the cache was seeded from the mirror `maintenance/README.md` documents, fetched through the GitHub blobs API.
